ParlaMint-DK_20150513130107	Then it's the Minister of Finance.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513130110	Thank you. It is basically government policy and also a result that we are slowly achieving that we have tried to send Denmark as gently through the economic crisis that has been going on for seven years as possible. We have therefore kept our hands on the labour market. We have invested quite massively in growth and employment, so that fewer people become unemployed and so that more people get to work or get an education. At the same time, as the questioner also knows, the government has concluded a number of political agreements with the SF and Enhedslisten to mitigate the consequences of the overly harsh phasing-in of the Danish People's Party and the VK government's unemployment benefit reform that appeared in recent years. I'd like to sign for that. Together, we have carried out a temporary extension of the daily allowance period of 1⁄2 years, we have launched a special effort for the unemployed at the risk of using up the benefit entitlement, and we have reached an agreement on emergency jobs. In addition, we have introduced the training allowance, the temporary labour market allowance and, most recently, the cash allowance, which has provided a temporary substitute for those who risk being out of support as a result of the VK government and DF's unemployment benefit reform. The total cost impact of the agreements I have just mentioned amounts to over 4 billion. kr., and that's including funds for cash benefits. It is not technically possible to draw up a total consumption balance for the funds included in the estimate of the over 4 billion. kr., and the Minister for Employment has also previously informed the questioner about this. This is due to the fact that, in the context of the cost-related consequences, counter-productive minor costs of other benefits are included, and some initiatives, including temporary labour market benefits and cash benefits, have not yet been completed. Finally, a number of initiatives do not appear on separate financial accounts and therefore consumption cannot be separated. But I can tell you that the Minister for Employment in the answer to questions to the Employment Committee, which is called No. 114, of 21 January 2015, has calculated the consumption of the schemes where possible, and so I would refer the questioner to the answer if the questioner wants a more detailed consumption statement.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513142404	Mrs Jane Heitmann for reading the question.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513142406	Thank you. On the basis of the answers to Question No 1, I should like to make a few comments. S 1158 Will the Minister then state what has changed in concrete terms, in the view of the Minister, since there should be no IC stop at Langeskov Station?
ParlaMint-DK_20150513142420	The minister.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513142421	As part of the political agreement of November 2010 concluded by the then government, the Left and the Conservatives, the Social Democrats, the Danish People's Party, the SF, the Radical Left and the Liberal Alliance, it was decided to reopen Langeskov Station. However, the agreement does not specify which train service Langeskov should have. The operating concept has only been defined later by DSB. The general starting point for the train service in Denmark on the routes for which DSB is responsible is therefore the requirements defined by the transport contract between the Ministry of Transport and DSB and which Banedanmark's channel allocation allows. Within this framework, it is DSB's responsibility to organise the roadmaps. Therefore, the contract with DSB does not require the precise roadmap in the form of e.g. precise minutes, or which trains, such as: Lightning trains, intercity trains and regional trains must be run. It is a principle that has been applied in the contracts with DSB, also when the VK government signed the contract with DSB, which applied from 2005 to 2014. It is a division of labour chosen to ensure that a long contract does not prevent flexibility in current traffic planning, where passengers' demand will change over the years. It is the case that the station in question will be located on the main line of East-West traffic, and therefore, in planning the operation of the station, consideration must of course be given to the expected passenger base in Langeskov, but it is also necessary to consider the impact on the trains in part of the country with the large passenger flows. DSB has then investigated this and DSB has used the traffic agency's forecasts. These forecasts show that Langeskov is expected to receive 258,000 travelers annually. According to DSB, it is significantly fewer travellers than at a number of regional and regional stations on Zealand and fewer than all current IC stations east of Lillebælt. It is clear that IC service by Langeskov will provide attractive direct connection with short travel times between Langeskov and IC stations at Zealand and a number of IC stations in Jutland, but IC service by Langeskov will also mean that about a third, approx. 2.1 million travelers between Odense and Nyborg will be extended by approx. 3 minutes in each direction. Overall, it is an extended journey time of more than 100,000 hours a year. The 2016 roadmap is designed to ensure that the lightning trains overtake one IC system in Odense, allowing passengers to switch from the IC train to the lightning train and thus achieve a travel time saving of 20 minutes for travellers from Esbjerg, Kolding and Sønderborg to Copenhagen. This will not be possible if the IC train is to stop in Langeskov. Further studies show that IC service by Langeskov will reduce travel time for the few, but vice versa extend travel time for the many. It has therefore been clear from the assessment by DSB that an IC service by Langeskov Station will have negative consequences for the vast majority of customers in public transport, and on this basis DSB has chosen regional and regional service by Langeskov. I am well aware that there are people in and around Langeskov who are disappointed in that decision. That is why I have also gone into dialogue with the Consumer Council Think and the Passenger Pulse to clarify the matter, and I think it would be good to have some new independent eyes on this matter.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513143445	The Minister of Transport.

This is, however, an unconditional condition for considering at all that the factual basis is in place because the airport is a private undertaking and such a requirement is a significant interference with the airport's right to self-determination. Because there are significant State interests linked to the shale gas, the State, in agreement with the Municipality of Frederikshavn, has taken over the further handling of the matter under the EIA rules. The Ministry of the Environment has therefore now taken over the task of deciding on Total's current use of new chemicals and any future notifications of changes to the project for the current drilling. It will be the Danish Nature Agency that deals with the matter, and in concrete terms this means that the Danish Nature Agency must decide whether the new chemicals require a new EIA statement. ParlaMint-DK_20150513150322	The Minister for the Environment.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513150324	The need to reduce nitrogen to the Danish water environment has been calculated by DHI and Aarhus University to be 7.860 tonnes of nitrogen. With a new targeted regulation, we expect more nitrogen to be added in some areas, while the nitrogen load to more sensitive areas must be reduced. The regulation of nitrogen will thus be more intelligent, more targeted and, overall, we will be able to benefit the environment and the industry at the same time. However, as the effort needs need to be analysed in greater detail, and as the policy decisions on effort needs and targeted regulation have not been taken now, I cannot say whether the government will postpone reductions or set additional efforts. For good reasons, I cannot decide beforehand on the conclusions of a committee of officials where we do not yet know the conclusions.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513151115	The debate is open. Minister of Employment.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513151138	It was a unanimous Parliament, which back in April 2014 adopted the special law on lately diagnosed ptsd. Then we agreed that we have a responsibility for our Danish soldiers because they are doing a very special and special job on behalf of all of us. A united Parliament wanted to do something very special for our soldiers, because we could see that some of them get scars on their souls from their work. The adoption included only soldiers, but when the law was drafted the special law was extended to cover other government employees who have also been sent to areas of conflict, for example: Police officers, investigators sent through the Ministry of Justice. I think that actually made sense because these missions were in the same area and in the same situations as the soldiers. In March 2014, in agreement, we adopted the law on compensation and compensation for former soldiers and other government employees with what we call late-diagnosed ptsd, and we did right in that. What Enhedslisten wants is to extend the circle to all public and private employees, and whether you have developed ptsd as a result of deployment to conflicting areas or during work in Denmark. I think it is very important that we stick to the fact that soldiers and other state workers sent to war zones are a very special group and that we must therefore maintain the law as it is. I would like to try to explain that a little more. The purpose of the special law was quite clear. What we wanted at the time was to ensure that soldiers are given the opportunity of compensation, like others who have developed ptsd as a result of the work, even though the soldiers are also diagnosed late. The special law merely implies that the obligation to provide evidence for this particular group is relaxed. This means that the soldiers have the opportunity to gain recognition of ptsd, although it has been several years since the individual has been sent out and had traumatizing influences during the broadcast. In these cases, the requirement for documentation is then a psychiatric specialist who considers that it is likely that the disease has arisen in time with the influences that have occurred in connection with the broadcast. It turns out, unfortunately, that the soldiers who come home with violent scars on their souls are at a particular risk, and they have not always contacted either doctor or authorities, even if they had the symptoms. This means that some people can go all alone with the disease for years before they get a real diagnosis and before they are taken care of and their unhappy situation. The personal delineation of the Special Act must therefore be seen in the light of the fact that soldiers are a very special group in our labour market. Their working conditions are quite incomparable to the working conditions which generally characterise our Danish labour market. The same applies to other state employees who are sent to the same duties as soldiers in war zone in the service of Denmark, for example. personnel standing at the opening of mass graves or having border guarding tasks. It is therefore a matter of relaxing the requirement for documentation because of the very special nature of the work and because it is a very special situation. As I said at the outset, this has proved to be what a united Parliament wanted to do, and I think that, as I said, was quite reasonable. The Labour Injuries Board has stated that almost 200 cases have been recognised under the special law during the first years of the special law. It is soldiers who have become ill as a result of their service and who have now been compensated for the psychological damage they have suffered. Once that has been established, I also think it is important to bear in mind that the special law is based on the ordinary work injury systems that we have. All workers, not only soldiers, can be recognised as an occupational disease and thus receive compensation under the Work Damage Insurance Act. This also applies to the initial ptsd, including the delay, which has a slightly longer disease development. It is my clear view that employees who have not been assigned to war zones do not have the same risk of going to an undiagnosed ptsd for years as has been shown to former soldiers, and that is why the special law only covers former soldiers and other state employees. The special law thus expressed a broad political desire for the State as an employer to assume responsibility for securing this particular group of employees. It is certainly my impression that by far the vast majority of employers in Denmark assume a responsibility which helps to reduce the risk that educators, firemen, and whatever else may be, develop the unhappy thing that ptsd is because of their work. Finally, I would like to conclude by saying that if we adopted the Enhedslisten's proposal, it would mean that the special law would be extended to the whole labour market, and that would really mean that we would put the ordinary law on occupational injury insurance out of effect. In fact, I do not think that is the right way to go. I do not think that we should generally extend the law so that it becomes part of the general system of occupational injury, as I believe has also been shown by what I have said here. So the government can't support Enhedslisten's proposal.

What did the investigation say? Among other things: the conclusion stated, and I quote: "PTSD may develop later than six months after exposure to a traumatic event (delayed PTSD). In the analysis reference is made to studies showing that about 25 percent of all PTSD cases are delayed PTSD. Persons who develop delayed PTSD have mostly experienced some symptoms of PTSD within the first months of the traumatic event. Finally, the explanation says: " Delayed PTSD occurs more frequently among professional professional groups (e.g. soldiers) than among others. The study was about people who had a ptsd diagnosis on different backgrounds. It's about people like policemen, prison workers, social educators, paramedics. It is clear from the bill that it will be the Labour Damage Board, which will pay any compensation, so that it will be financed through the AES. I would add that it is a relatively small amount, but whatever it is, it is a form of equality between those who come from a traumatic incident that leads to ptsd, no matter what job they contest. This is important when we look at the Danish Work Damage Act and many other laws. That's logic for caged hens. In other words, I believe that the law should be adapted so that the objective requirements already known from the legislation on occupational injury are maintained, so that persons exposed to the same incidents and the same documentation difficulties are put on an equal footing, regardless of whether they are soldiers, policemen, firemen or other professional groups. After the adoption in connection with the question and before the adoption of the law from the beginning of 2014, the Labour Damage Board started with a new practice in which they resumed a number of cases on the basis of the analysis of delayed ptsd. When they take them up, this means that the requirement for injured persons to be able to provide medical information prepared in connection with onset of symptoms, as a starting point within 6 months, will lapse. That is clear from the Minister's reply to question No. 13 in the course of the examination of the law, in which she says: On the basis of the analysis of late ptsd, the diagnosis is delayed with a course of illness in which the injured person has had some symptoms within 6 months and full onset of illness within a few years, recorded on the list of occupational diseases and can be recognized administratively by the Danish Employment Injuries Office. The relaxed practice of recognising delayed ptsd has been in force throughout the labour market since the summer of 2013. That's right. I agree with those who say that of course you can have late ptsd among all people; it is a question of documentation requirements. The law should therefore naturally cover all professional groups who have been exposed to something particularly traumatising and have developed symptoms, etc. I have fought from the outset for the demands of the veterans, but following that I have talked to them that they would not be treated as a special group. ParlaMint-DK_20150513160139	Thank you. The proposal here is justified by the fact that, according to the proposers, studies show that atypical and often very loose forms of employment for pilots and cabin crews have negative consequences for, among others. safety. According to the proposal, particularly non-permanent pilots on low-cost airlines should find it difficult to quit, for example if they scream or suffer from disease, even though they are formally obliged to do so in the interests of the safety of air passengers. And that is why the proposers believe that a larger proportion of the crew on board the aircraft should be directly employed by the airline. That is how I see the proposal, but of course we have the opportunity to hear the background to it when they are given the floor. We have had a recent consultation, in March, on the subject here, low-cost airlines and safety, and on the purely factual basis I have received no indications that there should be definite security problems for the low-cost airlines operating in Denmark. The same is true of the reports I have received from the European Union. However, as I also said at the conciliation meeting, and as I will repeat today, I agree that it is necessary to examine in more detail whether these new, changing company structures that are in the aviation industry and are constantly evolving can have some impact on safety in the future, and that action should be taken in the European Union, because it is the whole of the European Union that they operate. The European Aviation Safety Agency, EASA, has, inter alia: at Denmark's request, therefore, an investigation has been carried out into whether the design of the safety rules corresponds to the development of aviation business designs, business models and crew cultures. EASA shall also examine whether developments pose or may pose a risk to the safety of air carriers and to the employment of crew members. It is the case that Denmark is represented in the analysis group itself at the Danish Road Traffic Authority. It has been set in motion and I believe that we should wait for the outcome of the investigation in EASA on the consequences of these new aviation business structures before deciding, and of course also, what action should be taken in the light of these safety concerns. Then there is also a explanatory statement in the proposal which refers to an EU rule which says that at least 50%. aircraft mechanics must be directly employed in aircraft workshops. And according to the proposal, this 50-percent rule is justified by a desire to increase safety. In this context, it should be noted, for the sake of good order, that it is true that there is already a similar 50-percent rule for aircraft workshops in the European Union, but it is not unconditional. The unconditional requirement is that the workshop organisation must demonstrate that it has sufficient staff. In a guide, the EU has stated that the requirement for sufficient staff to ensure the stability of the organisation is considered acceptable if at least 50% of the staff are employed. staff are employed by the organisation. It is therefore not excluded that the requirement for sufficient staff can also be met by other means. As I read the background to the proposal, the desire is also to reduce the number of temporary and looser forms of employment, thereby increasing job security in the industry in general. In other words, to combat social dumping in aviation. I have to say that I fully agree that the problems of the aviation industry in the European Union are largely characterised by regulatory shopping. What we have seen has become such a race against the bottom, where only low wages and consequently bad employment conditions are competing only for certain and, unfortunately, growing sectors of the industry. This motion for a resolution refers to the report on social dumping and regulatory shopping in aviation published in March of this year. It has broad support from the industry, trade unions, ministries and companies, and it points to a number of areas which will ensure better market conditions. For example, it should be clearer which rights an employee is entitled to. Today there are examples of companies speculating on the forced transfer of employees from country to country. In general terms, I agree with a rule that 75%. be directly employed, will make it easier to identify who is the actual employer and thus who is responsible for pay and working conditions in relation to the employees. That is why I must say that the starting point for me in this matter is that I am in favour of looking at the proposal more closely. As you know, the companies that particularly use fixed-term contracts are not Danish, I think most of them are familiar with that. And I therefore assume that we can all agree that such a 75% direct recruitment rule makes sense only at EU level, and that naturally requires the initiative of the European Commission and not least the agreement of the other EU countries. In this respect, it is important to be aware that there may also be costs associated with such a rule. We will certainly be told that, at least if such a proposal is being debated, among other things. It will, of course, be argued that the rule will make EU companies less flexible. It may be harder for companies to adapt to market activity over seasons, and we will also hear the argument that it will be harder to react to sudden market changes compared to third country airlines. It is therefore important that we carefully consider such a rule, so that we have the answers to the questions that will certainly come and also have the answers to why it should be 75% and why it should not be 50%, as we know from aircraft workshops, or perhaps why it should not be even more so that we have examined it completely in depth. We must also examine whether we should use the same mechanism as we know from the aircraft workshops, i.e. whether it should be an unconditional requirement or whether we should be able to meet this requirement in other ways, for example by guaranteeing temporary workers, contract staff and other conditions similar to those of permanent staff. So, overall, I would say that I can certainly see positive elements in the proposal, but there are also a number of challenges at this stage. It is the case that the government cannot go out and give its firm support to this resolution, as it stands, but we are in full swing, including at EU level, to clarify the subject. I look forward to the continued discussions both here in Parliament and, of course, also in the committee of this proposal, which really addresses a subject which needs to be addressed.

It is also a valid principle for drivers. If you're tired, don't drive a car. This also applies to pilots, especially for pilots who are responsible for other people's lives. And I trust that pilots have the responsibility to say that if you're unable to fly, you just don't fly.

These may be perishable goods which cannot withstand exposure to sunlight; transport of e.g. medicines require special equipment, etc. So these are the concerns I have about this. But that does not say that it is the case that you cannot become wiser. And I can say that, as the Enhedslisten himself also refers to, the government will follow very closely the experience gained by our Swedish colleagues after they have recently introduced rules on the detention of the whole wagon train and not just the tractor, as we do in Denmark. This question, that is to say, about compliance with the rules on the carriage of goods, must be taken very seriously, and we must continue to do so. This applies to the overall rules, and it applies to efforts to avoid social dumping at all. And it has to be said that developments are constantly taking place, so we must also constantly develop in relation to the answers that have to come from here. However, this must of course be done in a way that is proper and correct and that does not introduce anything that has legal concerns. Such rules should not, of course, be introduced into the Danish Road Transport Act. And that is why the government cannot support the resolution, although we share the very intention of the proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513171431	Thank you, Minister. Then we move on to the rapporteurs. The first is sir. Kristian Pihl Lorentzen as rapporteur for the Left.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513171443	With this motion for a resolution, the Enhedslisten will have amended the Road Traffic Act so that it will be possible for the police to detain the whole wagon train, i.e. both the tractor and the trailer, if goods are carried out in breach of the law, including for example. in the event of a breach of the driving rest time rules or cabotage rules. The amendment is intended to be implemented in such a way that there is no retention of the goods themselves, which can be reloaded for other transport. Left sees no need for such a change of law, and we actually think that Enhedslisten is setting up to shoot gray sparrows with guns. As a result of past tightening up of legislation, which we have jointly done here in the Danish Parliament, the police already have the opportunity to withhold the attractive unit until the fines imposed are secured and there is already co-responsibility for freight forwarders and transport buyers. The issue has been dealt with in the report of the Cabotage Committee in 2013, and the Ministry of Justice stated in this regard, and I quote: "When it comes to the retention of the trailer/whole wagon train, it raises a number of legal questions. This applies, among other things, to: in relation to the fundamental principle of proportionality, as the fines could be covered by the value of the part of the wagon (even the truck) that can already be held today. Furthermore, there is one consideration to take to the owner of the cargo that may be in the trailer/car train. It is a quote from the report of the Cabotage Committee, page 29. The Left agrees that the retention of the whole vehicle would be unreasonably and legally questionable. Let me take an example. A Danish transport company buys a foreign subcontractor to pick up one of the Danish company's trailers with cargo in Aarhus to drive it to Munich. It could, for example, be done. be a chilled railer with perishable foods. The police can today check the driving-rest-time 28 days back in time by a roadside check. It will therefore give the totally unfair situation that the Danish company has its trailer detained for an infringement that may have been committed many days before they have hired the foreign carrier. This will lead to legal uncertainty afterwards. As far as co-responsibility is concerned, there is already a legal co-responsibility for freight forwarders and transport buyers under Section 23 of the Criminal Code in cases where freight forwarders have been aware of or have contributed to the illegal operation. We consider this to be sufficient. It is quite unreasonable in my world if you are to be punished for something you have not committed or had no influence on. It is just before you get the impression that Enhedslisten's real purpose of detention is to give a penalty in itself. However, the objective purpose of the detention must be to guarantee payment of the fines. There it is quite enough to detain the truck itself, i.e. the towing unit. The Left notes that the necessary tools already exist if the Danish transport company, freight forwarder or transport buyer has actually influenced the offence. The situations that Enhedslisten encounters with this proposal are therefore the situations in which the Danish transport buyer has had no influence on the wrongdoings committed. So Enhedslisten is really in the process of introducing chain liability by the back way, and the Left cannot support that. Finally, I would like to welcome the fact that the number of heavy vehicle offences has actually decreased, although controls have intensified over the last two to three years. This is a welcome development. That is why it makes no sense to adopt further restrictions when the rules and controls that we have adopted here by the Danish Parliament actually work in practice. The Left is not helping to shoot grey sparrows with guns, which is why we cannot support the motion for a resolution.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513180006	Thank you. This bill is part of the growth package, of which Dansk Folkeparti is also a part. ParlaMint-DK_20150513180210	Thank you. One of the major challenges in our outermost regions and rural areas is, as we have discussed it many times, the many unsellable homes. We have therefore heard the municipalities what it takes to improve the system and their wishes have been met in this proposal. Therefore, the scheme is now being made even more flexible so that, firstly, the permit follows the home instead of the owner, which allows the permit to be followed by a sale. Secondly, we are now extending the scheme so that municipalities can issue flex housing permits where there is free movement between the full-year and free-time status, without the permit being withdrawn, unlike the current scheme, where a holiday home that has changed its status as a leisure home can return to the holiday home again, but then the permit lapses, and then needs to be re-examined. It is about the people who live in our outermost regions and rural areas and their opportunities for fulfilling their dreams. It is therefore extremely important that we can now make it easier to sell houses in the countryside by making it more attractive as a buyer to buy a dwelling that is not bound by its being used for a specific purpose. If the flex housing scheme can get a positive spiral going, it will be of great benefit to our outermost regions, and I think this bill can help. We will see an increase in the turnover of housing, which will help to ensure that the houses are maintained to a certain extent. Another thing that we Radicals are very satisfied with in the draft law is the derogation from the land-zone permit requirement of the Plan Act when re-engaging the full-year use of a dwelling, so no matter how long a dwelling has enjoyed leisure status, flexible housing, both present and future, is exempted from the requirement of a land-zone permit. As an extra small curl on the tail, the municipalities are given the opportunity to give binding prior consents for flex housing permits to persons other than the current owner of the dwelling, which is also very good. The last welcome side benefit is the expectation of a smaller administrative burden on the municipalities, which now only has to grant one authorisation once. The scheme must be evaluated after two years, so that there is an opportunity to see whether further action is necessary, and we think that is the right thing to do as well. The Act is intended to enter into force on 1 July 2015, and the Radical Left endorses the bill. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513181222	I would like to start by thanking the Danish Parliament for its favourable treatment of the bill. It shows that we've hit right. The comments that have been made also show that this change was needed. Let me just start by saying that in 2013 the government introduced the flex housing scheme, which was based on a desire to provide municipalities with a tool to deal with the difficulties associated with a declining demand for housing in rural areas. The amendment of the law laid down the practice which already existed among some municipalities. There was a miskemask around with weekend permits and everything else that was developed to allow homeowners to use full-year residences for leisure purposes. However, the flexible housing scheme went one step further, and with a flexible residence permit in hand, the homeowners shall not seek permission under the Building Act or the Housing Regulation Act when the full year's use is later resumed. In fact, the flexible housing scheme received a very warm reception from the Things last time. In order to promote application and strengthen demand, we concluded a further agreement in the context of the growth package of June 2014. We made a broad agreement on the extension of the scheme, and the agreed extension should allow the municipality to allow the permit to follow the residence rather than the owner personally. It is a permit granted by a sale and it also gives the flex accommodation a greater guarantee of the transferability of the dwelling, since it can be expected that it is also a flex accommodation when it is sold and thus provides a basis for achieving better conditions for financing purchases. Here I can add that a flex accommodation can also be financed by up to 80%. mortgage lending, speaking of which, sir. Lars Dohn mentioned that, contrary to a summer house, where it is only 60%, you can therefore get funding on equal terms with a holiday home. As the Members of the House have also noted, the proposed bill contains a number of additional measures that result from the government's growth initiative. It can be said that the flex housing scheme has been introduced to stimulate the housing market and activity in rural areas. I have therefore also followed with great interest the development of application and have continuously picked up the practical experience of the administration of the former flexible housing system. Studies have shown that there was indeed a desire to strengthen the security of the long-term use of housing and thus also to strengthen the possibility of marketability and the provision of financing for housing. That is why I am also pleased that today we can jointly support this ambitious bill, which builds on practical experience and responds to the wishes of citizens, users, municipalities and real estate brokers alike to improve the arrangements. We therefore wish to stress with this bill the special features of the flexible housing scheme and to remove the uncertainties which may have arisen. It can then be said that this also influences the ability of homeowners to use the permit in terms of how to buy. The draft law exempts flex housing in rural areas entirely from the land-zoning permit requirements of the Plan Act when the full-year use is resumed later, and the exemption applies regardless of how long the flex residence has been used as a leisure home, and it also includes, of course, existing flex housing. No one asked that question, but there are now some 400 flexes, and so they are also covered by the amendment proposed here. The draft law also provides for the possibility of granting a flex housing permit which does not automatically lapse when the use of the house is resumed for the whole year. This means that you can later choose to use the dwelling as a leisure home again without having to apply for a new permit. I think that is a very important part. Finally, the draft law takes special account of those who could be potential buyers. For municipalities are now being given the opportunity to give binding prior consent for a flexible permit. This could not be done in the second system, which we are now improving. This means that people who are interested in buying will have complete certainty about the right of use in the future, even before they sign the final note. To sum up, it can be said that the bill provides municipalities with a number of new tools to strengthen the demand for flex housing in our rural areas. Furthermore, enlargement gives each municipality a very great deal of freedom as to how the scheme is to be managed locally, and it is my expectation that these initiatives will create a marked increase in demand for flex housing, and it must also help to increase activity in rural areas. So thank you for all the good comments. I look forward to joint good committee work.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513181756	Our forests are important for the diversity of our species and our habitat types and for our biodiversity. They are because of the nature of Denmark as a forest land, and therefore many of our species are found in forest. And they are because the forest area today, despite the fact that it is now in progress, only covers approx. 14% of our area. Nature is under pressure and we must make an effort to stop the decline in biodiversity, to which we have committed ourselves both within the European Union and the United Nations. That is why I am very pleased that there has been a great political interest in biodiversity and the forests of the state. State ownership of forests and nature is an important instrument of natural policy. Even now, biodiversity is the main objective of 12%. of the forest area of the Ministry of the Environment, and it also has an important place on the rest of the land. But forests also provide other benefits for society. The forests provide the framework for millions of good experiences, whether we go, run, bikes or go to school. The forests protect our drinking water and clean our air, and the forests deliver sustainable building material and energy. The Government agrees that efforts must be made in forests to halt the decline in biodiversity. That is why we have also set aside over 50 million. kr. to biodiversity in the forests of Nature Plan Denmark. It is necessary to have precise knowledge of where and how to take action when we have to prioritise between the many considerations, even when it comes to biodiversity, where it does not matter at all which species we will benefit and where and how. And we do not know in concrete terms where the most important biodiversity is found in forests and which instruments are the most effective. It is important for the government that we act on an informed basis so that efforts are targeted at the right areas and means of action. Therefore, the government with Nature Plan Denmark has launched a series of analyses that will make us wiser on how best to improve efforts for biodiversity and on what the cost will be. We're among other things. by providing a comprehensive national mapping of particularly valuable public and private forests, so that we can create an overview of where the species and habitat types are. There will also be a study of where the particularly endangered species are located on the territory of the Ministry of the Environment and what specific measures are needed to stop their decline. Finally, we are giving the Danish Nature Agency's existing designations of the natural forest areas from 1992 a review. The first results of the analyses are ready mid 2016. In the light of those analyses, there will be a solid basis for discussing further efforts and possible target-setting on the areas of the Ministry of the Environment. I also note that the authors of the motion for a resolution we are debating write in the comments on the proposal that, and I quote, it is carefully examined and analysed which areas are best suited to Danish forests and which initiatives need to be taken to increase biodiversity, as it may be assumed that untouched forest is not the only method. And that's where we agree. That is why we must also do things in the right order. The government has also concluded an agreement with SF and Enhedslisten to put extra turbo on the effort for biodiversity in the forests, so that we can get started more quickly in helping the endangered species in the forests and the valuable forest nature. We are doing this by strengthening the level of knowledge and by taking immediate action to ensure biodiversity in private forests. We will set aside another 6 million. kr. to strengthen efforts for untouched private forest, and that is on top of the 12 million. DKK, which has already been allocated with Nature Plan Denmark, and DKK 2 million. kr. to improve the knowledge base of forest biodiversity. The authors of this motion for a resolution propose that we reduce our protected section 3 areas in proportion to the interpretation of untouched forest. So they'll bet everything on the swings so they don't have to start the carousels. Unfortunately, however, it does not work that way when it comes to strengthening biodiversity. I do not agree that it is possible to achieve the same biodiversity by exchanging our protected § 3 nature with untouched forest. Both in the forest and in § 3 protected light-open natural areas, there is significant biodiversity that needs to be protected, but there are quite different habitat types and different species. I have also noted that the resolution assumes that the cost will be 10 million. kr. a year. That estimate has been made on the wrong basis, which I was also given the opportunity to discuss with at least some of you last week at the meetings we held. The 10 million. kr. equals 20%. of the net revenue of the Danish Nature Agency in 2013 from all forestry operations on all land minus all fixed costs. However, first of all, there is a difference between the loss of income, depending on the type of forest concerned, and the areas identified by the researchers as particularly valuable to biodiversity are, at the same time, the areas on which there are the highest values of wood. Secondly, there are a large number of costs which would still have to be covered, such as property taxes and road maintenance. Thirdly, and perhaps most importantly, if the untouched forest is to really benefit biodiversity, we will have to take active care of a lot of places with grazing, grubbing-up, etc. The real cost of planting deciduous forest equal to 20 per cent. of the Ministry of the Environment's forest area as untouched forest without cutting would be 30-40 million. kr. the existing 5000 hectares of untouched forest have already been taken into account by the Ministry of the Environment, which has already been laid down. In addition, the annual cost of nature care, which can quickly amount to more than a million. If the Ministry of the Environment is to finance a lost income of that magnitude, it will even go very hard on nature elsewhere. This could also mean that the forests of the Ministry of the Environment would also not be able to offer the good framework for open-air life as we want them to be, and the one-sided efforts of the government are not willing to do. The government wants a Denmark where natural pearls and biodiversity are addressed in the forests, but the government also wants forests where sustainable wood is produced for energy and where new forests create coherence in the landscape, more opportunities for outdoor life, counteract climate change and increase the protection of our groundwater. Forests that benefit and benefit both present and future generations are the vision of the forestry programme that we are putting the finishing touches to. If our forests are to meet all their wishes, it requires that we have a solid basis for decision-making so that we can take well-considered and balanced decisions, not a one-sided and hasty decision that is not funding for. The government cannot therefore support the motion for a resolution.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513193727	Thank you. The purpose of this proposal is to take 20 per cent. 25,000 ha of the Danish state-owned forests in order to ensure better conditions for increasing biodiversity in Denmark. The proposal also calls on the government to offer the same scheme to private forest owners so that they can voluntarily conclude agreements to contribute. There is no doubt that, with the implementation of the content of this proposal, we will see a great and also positive impact on biodiversity, and even relatively quickly, some clear results. The World Wildlife Fund estimates that an increase of 25,000 hectares for untouched forest will have positive effects for 65%. of the redlisted species in Denmark. In addition, there are also more experts who point out that the focus on untouched forest provides a more effective increase in biodiversity than for example. with the focus we have on section 3 areas. It is also much cheaper to make efforts in the forests. In other words, we will have more biodiversity for less money. I think that's what you can call a win-win situation in old-fashioned Danish. ParlaMint-DK_20150513201938	The debate is open, and I will give the floor again to the Minister for the Environment.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513201956	As we have just discussed during the discussion of the previous proposal, the forest is very valuable, not least for our biodiversity, and therefore I would like to welcome once again the fact that we now have another proposal on biodiversity in our forests. The resolution of the Enhedslisten that we are debating now means that the whole of Gribskov and Esrum Lake and the protected areas east of the lake and narrow forests are being interpreted into what is called Nature National Park and untouched forest with grazing. On a number of points, I see some problems with this proposal, not least again with regard to funding, where the draft law here proposes that the conversion of the forest should be financed by cutting the coniferous forest. One third of Gribskov consists of coniferous forest. If large parts of it are to be removed over a short period of time, as proposed in the proposal, it will have major consequences for biodiversity and open-air life in Gribskov. There are several of the rare species in Gribskov that depend on just old spruce, for example. Swallow and black woodpecker. A clearing of both young and old spruce will reset biodiversity on a large area, which it will take many years to restore. Such extensive grubbing-up will also have drastic consequences for the remaining forest areas when they are suddenly left open to the public's access to and experience of the forest. The motion for a resolution stresses the need for care in large parts of the forest. Among other things, it has to be done. make with a wide range of grassers as it stands, i.e.. horses, cows, deer and other large animals. Of course, the cost of care depends on how intensive it is. There is already forest grazing in Gribskov and in a number of other forests. The experience of this shows that grazing, which is envisaged here in the proposal, is relatively expensive. The Danish Nature Agency has calculated on the proposal and the income from all the conifers to be felled cannot even cover the cost of the increase for the first five years. On average, this will cost approx. 20 million kr. per year in lost income and care measures. In the first five years, however, expenditure will be higher because there will be set-up costs for e.g.: Fence. In concrete terms, 75 million will be available. kr. for the first 5 years, if 1400 ha of the total 1600 ha of spruce are cleared, but with the average annual cost of approx. 20 million kr. However, it will result in a significant loss, even in the first 5 years. The government will not deny that it may make sense to focus even more on biodiversity in parts of Gribskov, but as I also mentioned in connection with the previous resolution, B 144, it is important that we act on an enlightened basis so that we can target efforts on the right areas with the right means of action. Therefore, the government believes that any further efforts in Gribskov should await the analyses launched under Nature Plan Denmark • analyses, which by the way have now been strengthened by an agreement between the government, SF and Enhedslisten. These are analyses that will make us wiser on how to do what to improve efforts for biodiversity in forests. Furthermore, I believe that we can easily take account of biodiversity without having to establish a new type of protection category, as proposed here with Natural National Parks, and that is why the government cannot support this proposal. However, we welcome the fact that we have just reached an agreement with the SF and the Enhedslisten, which prioritises biodiversity in forests, as we also did with our Financial Act for 2015. State ownership of forests and nature is an important instrument of natural policy, which is why the state must, of course, make an effort to promote biodiversity. But we must do so on an enlightened basis. I am open to discussing in committee whether we can also find a common understanding with regard to this proposal, and that we, for example, should. We can allow Gribskov to be the subject of a special debate in the context of the follow-up to the analyses that we also agree to set in motion to see where priority should be given to action. I also believe that this would be the right order to do so in that we will have the analyses of where it would make the most sense to focus the efforts, and that we will then be able to see whether Gribskov could be the subject of a special discussion on the basis of the analyses.

So in many ways you just have to say that it is nice that there is more focus on biodiversity and that it is nice that there is more focus on untouched forest, but the Radical Left cannot endorse the proposal. However, we look forward very much to discussing it further in the course of the committee procedure.

ParlaMint-DK_20150513204704	Thank you to the Conservative rapporteur. Then we go to the draftsman of the opinion of the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, the Verification of Credentials and Immunities, Mr von der Vring. Per Clausen.
ParlaMint-DK_20150513204715	I understand from what the Minister for the Environment said earlier that she could not understand how I had come to the idea that the government did not want to commit further to establishing untouched forests. To that end, I would like to say, as a point 1, that the Minister can easily tear me out of that error by simply stating that we can, of course, make a report in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Policy, the first sentence of which is that we agree that more untouched forest should be established in the years to come than what has been planned so far. Paragraph 2: We will find out where it is best through objective, professional investigation and paragraph 3: We will agree on the funding. Many more words could be written about that. The point is, this is a very simple way of solving the error I might find myself in about government policy. It is not, of course, a question of denying that the government has adopted a nature plan; it is not about denying that, both in relation to the distribution of rural funds and in relation to changing financial laws, the government has concluded agreements which lead to more biodiversity and to a very limited extent also to more untouched forests; nor is it a denial that, in the final stages of the negotiations on a relatively small amount of funds left over in the financial negotiations, it goes without saying that it is relatively little money; otherwise, we would have done our job badly in the negotiations. But it's kind of forward-looking. And when I think it is a little important, it is of course because I know that there has been some discussion back and forth about it, but I can see that the conclusion, as I understand it, is that it is now agreed that Danish forests are in an unfavourable conservation situation. No matter what experts I try to seek out, and how eagerly and thoroughly I listen to what the Danish Nature Agency says, it is absolutely impossible for me to get anything else out of what they say, except that untouched forest, perhaps even untouched forest with grazing, is the most effective way to increase biodiversity in forests. I have not actually met anyone yet who can be said to understand this, who denies that this is the case. This does not mean that nature should not also be given priority in the open landscape. Nor does it mean that priority should not be given to converting some of what is now cultivated agricultural land to nature, so that we will have more biodiversity through years. It does not mean that, but it is just to say that I think it is beyond doubt that the forest and untouched forest is a good idea. And it is also true that when you talk about where to establish untouched forests, there may be biodiversity considerations that may point to doing so in certain places. Again I would say that I think it will be difficult to find experts who will deny that the proposal here is a very good one in relation to it, but it may also be the element of natural experience which, of course, also matters. It is clear that it will probably take many years, for example. leaving a fir forest in West Jutland as untouched forest becomes really exciting and really interesting, both in one way and another, so this is probably a long way along the right path. It is also true that in this area we have tried to use nature-like forestry, and it is true that this has an effect. It is clear that if you go from a forestry operation that is completely regardless of nature to a forestry operation that takes a little more account of nature, it will be a little better. But this proposal is therefore a proposal to proceed more radically. This is also the kind of discussion which sometimes arises, and which has arisen several times in relation to North Zealand, and in which we have also asked the Minister several times about what some people see as a very ruthless felling of very old trees, very large trees which are undoubtedly very worthwhile, but where one can ask the question whether it was perhaps also a place where one could say that one took a break in relation to it while doing the investigation work. It could also be a way of showing that one was actually very willing to deal with issues relating to biodiversity in forests and forest conservation. So that's the one thing we think this proposal is a very good one. The second and, of course, one can discuss whether it is appropriate and wise to establish and use state forests as a bridgehead in establishing untouched forests. After all, the consequence of the debate is that the cost will be borne by the owner, namely the State. However, it may also be a little strange to have that approach, and I would at least say as a socialist that I think it is a rather unpleasant approach that what is state-owned cannot take the lead. Well, I think that the fact that the state is taking the lead voluntarily after finding out how it is financed in some form of political conciliation is a very good idea, very sensible and very good. I must admit that I find it a little difficult to understand that the fact that the state treats its part of a future national park more favorably in relation to nature should cause others to withdraw. I might have a little bit of trouble understanding that, so I think this might fit very well into it. I would also like to say that there is no reason to hide the fact that in this motion for a resolution there is also a kind and mild and suppressed criticism of the whole of the national park idea, which has been the driving force in Denmark, where it has been based on the fact that no legislative restrictions were made in a national park on what the owners had to do, and then an agreement was reached on everything that could be agreed voluntarily, which quite often turned out to be very little. And then they called it a national park. There are not many places in the world where such things are called national parks, but we have chosen to do so in Denmark. In fact, if you used the Enhedslisten's approach to this, you might actually get an area in Denmark that approached some of what you also in other parts of the world call national parks, and we think that would be really good. I am not going to drag the debate much further into the long run. I do not have time for that, even if I wanted to, so it is quite free to say so, but just to say that I have the very clear expectation of the debate that has been on these two motions for resolutions today, that it will prove that in the forthcoming committee work we are able to conclude agreements which means that we jointly commit ourselves to having more untouched forest in the future, that this more untouched forest will not be established as a substitute for other nature, and that we will also find funding for what means that, in order to establish untouched forests, we will not have to restrict ourselves to other natural projects. This is certainly the starting point for the negotiations in the Enhedslisten. But everyone who knows about Enhedslistens and my practice in the Danish Parliament will know that we are always open and comprehensible and boundless modest in our demands for the end result. So, of course, I'm looking forward to getting a result out of this. If nothing else, we have a few days now, when we can relax and think about it. It can be good and favorable. Thank you.
